For as the vessel that is negligently moored, is very often (when the storm waxes violent) tossed by the water out of its shelter on the safest shore, so under the cloak of the Ecclesiastical office, I found myself plunged on a sudden in a sea of secular matters, and because I had not held fast the tranquillity of the monastery when in possession, I learnt by losing it, how closely it should have been held."<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> In <i>Moralia, sive Expositio in Job</i> ("Commentary on Job," also known as <i>Magna Moralia</i>), Gregory describes to the Bishop Leander the circumstances under which he became a monk.</li>
<li>"Illiterate men can contemplate in the lines of a picture what they cannot learn by means of the written word."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBarasch2013_93-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBarasch2013-93">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i>Age quod agis</i> (Do what you are doing).<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> Through the centuries, this would become a repeated maxim of Catholic mystics and spiritual directors encouraging one to keep focus on what one is doing in trying to serve the Lord.</li>
<li>"Repentance is weeping for what one has done and not doing what one weeps for."<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Memorials">Memorials</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Gregory_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Memorials">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Relics">Relics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Gregory_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Relics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Roma_2010_(5047768606).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Roma_2010_%285047768606%29.jpg/170px-Roma_2010_%285047768606%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="227" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Roma_2010_%285047768606%29.jpg/255px-Roma_2010_%285047768606%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Roma_2010_%285047768606%29.jpg/340px-Roma_2010_%285047768606%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2736" data-file-height="3648" /></a><figcaption>Altar of Saint Gregory in <a href="/wiki/St._Peter%27s_Basilica" title="St. Peter&#39;s Basilica">St. Peter's Basilica</a>, which contains the remains of Pope Gregory.</figcaption></figure>
<p>The relics of Saint Gregory are enshrined in <a href="/wiki/St._Peter%27s_Basilica" title="St. Peter&#39;s Basilica">St. Peter's Basilica</a> in Rome.<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Lives">Lives</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Gregory_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Lives">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In Britain, appreciation for Gregory remained strong even after his death, with him being called <i>Gregorius noster</i> ("our Gregory") by the British.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChamp2000ix_97-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChamp2000ix-97">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> It was in Britain, at a monastery in <a href="/wiki/Whitby_Abbey" title="Whitby Abbey">Whitby</a>, that the first full-length <a href="/wiki/Hagiography" title="Hagiography">life</a> of Gregory was written, <span title="circa">c.</span><span style="white-space:nowrap;">&#8201;713</span>, by a monk or, possibly, a nun.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAnonymous_Monk_of_Whitby1985_98-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnonymous_Monk_of_Whitby1985-98">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> Appreciation of Gregory in Rome and Italy itself, however, did not come until later. The first <i>vita</i> of Gregory written in Italy was not produced until <a href="/wiki/Johannes_Hymonides" title="Johannes Hymonides">Johannes Hymonides</a> (aka John the Deacon) in the 9th century.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (September 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Monuments">Monuments</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Gregory_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Monuments">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The namesake church of <a href="/wiki/San_Gregorio_al_Celio" class="mw-redirect" title="San Gregorio al Celio">San Gregorio al Celio</a> (largely rebuilt from the original edifices during the 17th and 18th centuries) remembers his work. One of the three oratories annexed, the oratory of Saint Silvia, is said to lie over the tomb of Gregory's mother.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (September 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In England, Gregory, along with <a href="/wiki/Augustine_of_Canterbury" title="Augustine of Canterbury">Augustine of Canterbury</a>, is revered as the apostle of the land and the source of the nation's conversion.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERichards1980260_99-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERichards1980260-99">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Gregory_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Music">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Italian composer <a href="/wiki/Ottorino_Respighi" title="Ottorino Respighi">Ottorino Respighi</a> composed a piece named <i>St. Gregory the Great</i> <i>(San Gregorio Magno)</i> that features as the fourth and final part of his <i>Church Windows</i> (<i>Vetrate di Chiesa</i>) works, written in 1925.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (September 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Feast_day">Feast day</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Gregory_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Feast day">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The current <a href="/wiki/General_Roman_Calendar" title="General Roman Calendar">General Roman Calendar</a>, revised in 1969 as instructed by the <a href="/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council" title="Second Vatican Council">Second Vatican Council</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-vatican.va_100-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vatican.va-100">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> celebrates Saint Gregory the Great on 3 September. Before that, it assigned his feast day to 12 March, the day of his death in 604. Following the imposition of <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XXIII" title="Pope John XXIII">Pope John XXIII</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Code_of_Rubrics" title="Code of Rubrics">Code of Rubrics</a> in 1961, celebration of Saint Gregory's feast day was made practically impossible, as John XXIII's reforms forbade the full observance of most feasts during Lent, during which 12 March invariably falls. For this reason, Saint Gregory's feast day was moved to 3 September, the day of his episcopal <a href="/wiki/Consecration" class="mw-redirect" title="Consecration">consecration</a> in 590,<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> as part of the liturgical reforms of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI" title="Pope Paul VI">Pope Paul VI</a>.
The <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Eastern Orthodox Church</a> and those <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Catholic_Churches" title="Eastern Catholic Churches">Eastern Catholic Churches</a> which follow the <a href="/wiki/Byzantine_Rite" title="Byzantine Rite">Byzantine Rite</a> continue to commemorate Saint Gregory on 12 March which is during <a href="/wiki/Great_Lent" title="Great Lent">Great Lent</a>, the only time when the <a href="/wiki/Divine_Liturgy_of_the_Presanctified_Gifts" class="mw-redirect" title="Divine Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts">Divine Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts</a>, which names Saint Gregory as its author, is used.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (September 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Other churches also honour Gregory the Great:
</p>
<ul><li>The <a href="/wiki/Lutheran_Church%E2%80%93Missouri_Synod" title="Lutheran Church–Missouri Synod">Lutheran Church–Missouri Synod</a> <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Lutheran)" title="Calendar of saints (Lutheran)">remember</a> him with a <a href="https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/commemoration" class="extiw" title="wikt:commemoration">commemoration</a> on <a href="/wiki/September_3" title="September 3">3 September</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/Evangelical_Lutheran_Church_in_America" title="Evangelical Lutheran Church in America">Evangelical Lutheran Church in America</a> <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Lutheran)" title="Calendar of saints (Lutheran)">remember</a> him with a commemoration on <a href="/wiki/March_12" title="March 12">12 March</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Episcopal_Church)" title="Calendar of saints (Episcopal Church)">Episcopal Church of the United States</a> honors him on <a href="/wiki/March_12" title="March 12">12 March</a><sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/Anglican_Church_of_Canada" title="Anglican Church of Canada">Anglican Church of Canada</a> <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Anglican_Church_of_Canada)" title="Calendar of saints (Anglican Church of Canada)">remember</a> him with a <a href="/wiki/Memorial_(liturgy)" title="Memorial (liturgy)">Memorial</a> on <a href="/wiki/September_3" title="September 3">3 September</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-prayerbook.ca_105-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-prayerbook.ca-105">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Gregory the Great is <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Church_of_England)" title="Calendar of saints (Church of England)">remembered</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_England" title="Church of England">Church of England</a> with a <a href="/wiki/Lesser_Festival_(Anglicanism)" title="Lesser Festival (Anglicanism)">Lesser Festival</a> on <a href="/wiki/September_3" title="September 3">3 September</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<p>A traditional <a href="/wiki/Procession" title="Procession">procession</a> is held in <a href="/wiki/%C5%BBejtun" title="Żejtun">Żejtun</a>, <a href="/wiki/Malta" title="Malta">Malta</a>, in honour of Saint Gregory (San Girgor) on Easter Wednesday, which most often falls in April, the range of possible dates being 25 March to 28 April.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (September 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
The feast day of Saint Gregory also serves as a commemorative day for the former pupils of <a href="/wiki/Downside_School" title="Downside School">Downside School</a>, called Old Gregorians. Traditionally, OG ties are worn by all of the society's members on this day.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (September 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Written_works">Written works</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Gregory_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Written works">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="References">References</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Gregory_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: References">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Gregory_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap mw-references-columns"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-TheGreat-2"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-TheGreat_2-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Gregory had come to be known as 'the Great' by the late ninth century, a title which is still applied to him. See <a href="#CITEREFMoorhead2005">Moorhead 2005</a>, p.&#160;1 </span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-Totila-12"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Totila_12-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Gregory mentions in Dialogue 3.2 that he was alive when <a href="/wiki/Totila" title="Totila">Totila</a> attempted to murder Carbonius, Bishop of <a href="/wiki/Populonia" title="Populonia">Populonia</a>, probably in 546. In a letter of 598 (<i>Register</i>, Book 9, Letter 1) he rebukes Bishop Januarius of <a href="/wiki/Cagliari" title="Cagliari">Cagliari</a>, Sardinia, excusing himself for not observing 1 Timothy 5.1, which cautions against rebuking elders. Timothy 5.9 defines elderly women to be 60 and over, which would probably apply to all. Gregory appears not to consider himself an elder, limiting his birth to no earlier than 539, but 540 is the typical selection. See <a href="#CITEREFDudden1905">Dudden 1905</a>, pp.&#160;3, notes 1–3 The presumption of 540 has continued in modern times – see for example <a href="#CITEREFRichards1980">Richards 1980</a> </span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-translator-14"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-translator_14-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The translator goes on to state that "<a href="/wiki/Paul_the_Deacon" title="Paul the Deacon">Paulus Diaconus</a>, who first writ the life of St. Gregory, and is followed by all the after Writers on that subject, observes that <i>ex Greco eloquio in nostra lingua ... invigilator, seu vigilant sonnet.</i>" However, Paul the deacon is too late for the first <i>vita</i>, or life.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-grēgorein-15"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-grēgorein_15-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The name is biblical, derived from <a href="/wiki/New_Testament" title="New Testament">New Testament</a> contexts: grēgorein is a present, continuous aspect, meaning to be watchful of forsaking Christ. It is derived from a more ancient perfect, egrēgora, "roused from sleep", of egeirein, "to awaken someone." see <a href="#CITEREFThayer1962">Thayer 1962</a> </span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-IIIorIV-17"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-IIIorIV_17-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Whether III or IV depends on whether <a href="/wiki/Antipope_Felix_II" title="Antipope Felix II">Antipope Felix II</a> is to be considered pope.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-Palpate-38"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Palpate_38-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">"<i>Palpate et videte, quia spiritus carnem et ossa non-habet, sicut me videtis habere</i>, or "touch me, and look; a spirit has not flesh and bones, as you see that I have." - <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://bible.oremus.org/?passage=Luke%2024:39&amp;version=nrsv">Luke 24:39</a> </span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-42"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-42">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The dictionary account is apparently based on <a href="/wiki/Bede" title="Bede">Bede</a>, Book II, Chapter 1, who used the expression "...impalpable, of finer texture than wind and air."</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-Later-52"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Later_52-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Later these deacons became cardinals and from the oratories attached to the buildings grew churches</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-HancVero-75"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-HancVero_75-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">"Hanc vero quam Lucas peccatricem mulierem, Ioannes Mariam nominat, illam esse Mariam credimus de qua Marcus septem daemonia eiecta fuisse testatur" (<i>Patrologia Latina</i> 76:1239)</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-Colgrave-78"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Colgrave_78-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">For the various literary accounts, see <a href="#CITEREFAnonymous_Monk_of_Whitby1985">Anonymous Monk of Whitby 1985</a>, p.&#160;157, n. 110 </span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-Earliest-84"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Earliest_84-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The earliest life written a generation earlier than Bede at Whitby relates the same story but in it the English are merely visitors to Rome questioned by Gregory (see Holloway, who translates from the manuscript kept at <a href="/wiki/Abbey_library_of_St._Gallen" class="mw-redirect" title="Abbey library of St. Gallen">St. Gallen</a>). The earlier story is not necessarily the more accurate, as Gregory is known to have instructed presbyter Candidus in <a href="/wiki/Gaul" title="Gaul">Gaul</a> by letter to buy young English slaves for placement in monasteries. These were intended for missionary work in England: See <a href="#CITEREFAmbrosiniWillis1996">Ambrosini &amp; Willis 1996</a>, p.&#160;71 </span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-BenPel-85"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-BenPel_85-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_I" title="Pope Benedict I">Benedict I</a> or <a href="/wiki/Pelagius_II" class="mw-redirect" title="Pelagius II">Pelagius II</a>.</span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>

Bowyer.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=An+English-Saxon+Homily+on+the+Birth-day+of+St.+Gregory%3A+Anciently+Used+in+the+English-Saxon+Church%2C+Giving+an+Account+of+the+Conversion+of+the+English+from+Paganism+to+Christianity&amp;rft.place=London&amp;rft.pub=W.+Bowyer&amp;rft.date=1709&amp;rft.au=%C3%86lfric&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Farchive.org%2Fdetails%2Fanenglishsaxonh01elstgoog%2Fpage%2Fn79&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3APope+Gregory+I" class="Z3988"></span></li>

</div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="navbox-group" style="background-color:gold;width:1%">Early Modern and<br /> Modern Era</th><td class="navbox-list-with-group navbox-list navbox-even hlist" style="width:100%;padding:0"><div style="padding:0 0.25em">
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Renaissance_Papacy" title="Renaissance Papacy">Renaissance Papacy</a> (1417–1534)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Reformation_Papacy" title="Reformation Papacy">Reformation Papacy</a> (1534–1585)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Baroque_Papacy" class="mw-redirect" title="Baroque Papacy">Baroque Papacy</a> (1585–1689)</li>

</div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="navbox-group" style="width:1%">Centuries</th><td class="navbox-list-with-group navbox-list navbox-odd" style="width:100%;padding:0"><div style="padding:0 0.25em">
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Christianity_in_the_1st_century" title="Christianity in the 1st century">1st</a></li>
